It’s a person

Joshua: Oh, man. Well, you’ve shared about this relationship you’ve cultivated with your double. You wanna say something about that? Or where do you feel like the next place you wanna go on your story?

Steven: Yeah. I’ll go to the next events, which are probably starting November, December last year. Maybe a little before that. When we’re in the Techniques of Transcendence course, Darren King, you and I are in that course, things really start to pick up, especially the telepathy, and it feels very intrusive. I am getting thoughts and feelings that are not mine. They don’t make any sense. They’re completely out of step, and it’s a person. And later I’ll discover it’s more than one.

Just take me at my word. This is what I experienced. I’m not telling anyone what is true, but this is what’s happening to me, right? So it doesn’t stop. I can’t rule it out. I can’t make it stop. Normally you can make your thoughts stop. I know how to meditate. Nothing stops it. And the more this goes, it starts to pick up, especially as I start to do a lot of the stuff in that course. Which is again, mostly personal development, removing distortions, trying to see the world as it is, letting go of judgment.

It doesn’t seem like those things should give you more extrasensory perception, but it does. That is what gives you extrasensory perception. They’re the same thing somehow. They’re tied together. And the desire to know, right? You have to be curious and want to know on some level, which I did.

So these things start reaching into my life, or people, right? And I don’t know what to do with it. It’s waking state, or I’ll wake up in the middle of the night in the middle of a conversation, or I’m having a dream, and I become lucid, and it’s like a quiz. Somebody’s grilling me with questions. “What does that mean to you?” They’re training my discernment. “Why did that scare you? What did you think about that? How do you know?”

And then I go, “Wait, what the hell? Who are you?” I was being puppeted, or it’s just sort of in a malleable state until I became lucid, and then I start to panic. And then it’s like I can feel the emotion, like an eye roll, like, “Ah, guess he’s awake. All right. Ugh. Party’s over. All right, everyone go home.” That happens. It keeps happening. And I’m like, “Wait, who are you? What’s going on?”

And then it’s in waking state, not just meditative state. Traditionally, it’s like when my mind is wandering. I’m going for a run. I’m doing chores. Sitting in the hammock. “Hey, what’s up?” So stuff like that. Or I feel a strong emotion, and it’s completely out of tune with what I am. It’s somebody wants to reach out to me, or they wanna talk to me, and I keep pushing them away. Naturally, I’m worried I’m going crazy. And I can’t figure out what’s wrong with me. I’m treating it that way.

And every time I push them away, I feel this intense sadness, like a betrayal, like they’re hurt. They feel rejected. But I don’t feel that way. I’m like, “Ugh, go away. What is this? These aren’t real thoughts. You’re not real.” And then I just feel this pain, like, “You jerk.” Or just, “I feel sad that you’ve pushed me away. I’m trying to talk to you.” And you can only do that so many times before you’re like, “Man, I feel like if there’s any truth to this, I feel like the asshole.”

I’ve already accepted that things are pretty much idealistic. I’m not in my body. I’m something beyond it. I couldn’t tell you what that was at that time. Why not? Why couldn’t there be non-physical things? And of course, this is when they would start reaching out. You’ve done the groundwork. You have the abilities. You’ve got enough world models that I’m not gonna just have a psychotic break right away pushing me to the edge here.

So I start to take it seriously. But it’s like a pretense. I’m gonna pretend to have a conversation in my head. I’ll just act like it’s just me. And the more I do, and the more I entertain it, the more real it becomes. And that’s pretty crazy. And then the experiences at night and meditation, they don’t stop, as you well know. So these are a recurring cast of characters.

And I wanna know at some point. A lot of this is intuitive. I just have feelings that important things are happening or what it means. Intuition is starting to go off the charts because I’m leaning into it, and I just have a feeling that I know these people. I know them. I already have feelings about them, but I can’t remember them. It’s very difficult to describe how jarring that is. Like, “Oh, yeah, I know who you are.” I could tell you exactly how I feel about you, what our relationship is like. I don’t have any memories. I don’t have a lot of knowledge. I just have a felt sense of who they are, and it’s familiar. That’s very jarring.

The white plain

Steven: And then I start to have liminal experiences, so waking up or falling asleep, that are extremely vivid. So not only do I see symbols and stuff in the visual field, this is not imaginary. I get those, too, the imaginary field also, the imagined visuals. But close your eyes and, you know, stare at something, you see an after image, and it’s painted in the blackness. I see stuff drawn like that. Sometimes the visuals snap into perfect clarity. I don’t know how to do that consciously, but that started happening, too. And mostly symbolic stuff. I realize we can communicate, but I’m in this weird state where I don’t know what the hell is happening to me. I’m falling apart constantly, but it’s not bad.

And then I start to have experiences at night where it’s very vivid. I think the one experience that I mentioned to you is the one story I’d tell before we move on to what’s going on now, is that I had a sense I would talk to somebody at night. I wanted to know, did I have a soul family? At this point, I’m pretty on board for that’s who this probably is. I think maybe they’re spirit guides. I don’t know, man. I’m just guessing, right? Deceased loved ones. Who knows? I don’t know who they are.

It’s difficult. It’s messy. It’s very frustrating, this form of communication, ’cause it feels like your own thoughts. It feels like writing dialogue in a book a lot of the time. It’s like I just know what the next line is. It feels like you’re talking to yourself. But when it’s really strong, this is what people would call telepathy, basically, the thoughts just come, and it’s a person talking to you.

And so this one night, I have an experience where I have a sense I’m gonna meet somebody. And so I wake up around 2:00 a.m., and I’m aware of my body, and I’m sitting in bed, and I’m awake. But I can feel, drawn into my imagined visuals, is a person and a scene. It’s a white plain. There’s a floor, but no ceiling and walls. And it’s a girl, and she’s standing there, and she’s talking to me. Just a little small talk like, “Hey, what’s up? Hey, good to see you,” this basic intro stuff.

And I’m awake. I’m not sleeping. I can move my body, and when I move my body or put my attention on my body, I start to wake up. And so it starts to dissolve, kind of like a dream dissolves. And so I’m putting my focus on this, but it’s crystal clear, and it’s stable, and it’s like somebody is drawing a scene and a person into your own imagination. It’s happening. I’m not making it happen. You can imagine the scene on your own. Imagine that being forced into your imagination, and it’s static. It’s happening whether you want it to or not. I feel like I can shut it down and turn it off if I want to, but it’s like a shared thing.

And so I’m just standing. I don’t have a body. I’m just floating in front of this person, dumbfounded, and she’s talking to me, and I’m not saying anything. Just sort of knocking on the glass like, “Hey. Hello. I’m trying to talk to you. Can you answer me?” And then I just say, “Holy shit, this is real. You are actually real. I can see you now.”

I guess you could call this astral projection. I don’t know. I don’t know the terms. But I was there. It was more than imagination, but less than a physical reality, right? It’s like I was being given an imagined space, and I had my senses, but they were like 50% volume, right? And so I stop paying attention to my body, and I’m focused in this space. Now we’re talking, and she’s laughing, and I start to panic because this is very scary. She’s like, “Don’t do that.” And I can feel the whole place start to dissolve, like waking up from a nightmare. And she’s like, “Don’t worry, you can’t do that. Hey, you’re fine. Don’t freak out. Just go with it. Nothing’s hurting you. You’re gonna be okay.”

So I try to. I keep pausing mid-conversation because I’m expecting it to not be real. And so I’m in that state for about four hours, and it’s stable. I can ask whatever I want. I can do whatever I want, and we talk. And this is basically like a sibling, like a twin. You can imagine a twin that wasn’t born. Just chose not to be born. But, “Hey, you can hear me now, so I miss you. Hello. Hey.”

And we talk about a lot of things. I have a lot of questions, as you might imagine, but I’m dumbstruck. And then, of course, there’s a second part of that story if you want me to tell it, but I think you get the gist. I get info dumped as well as conversation, just regular catching up. And yeah, I have a soul family of sorts, not just human. But you have a life beyond your life as well, and I think everyone does. If you’ve lived more than once, if you believe in reincarnation, you might have people from other lives. They’re there, and they’re not here, and once they can talk to you, yeah, they love you. They miss you, of course. “Hey, you’ve got a mailbox now. I’m gonna send you messages.” And that’s what was happening.

Holding the belief

Joshua: Sounds like a major relief. You’re getting all of these communications coming through and wondering if they’re real, wondering if they’re really your own thoughts, and then finally having this experience where you visually meet this person and feel as if you’re in their presence. You are in their presence. Your experience was you are in their presence. But they’re there all the time too, right? It’s just in that liminal state, you were able to meet them more fully.

Steven: Yeah, I was a real participant, and it was more rich, a more vivid experience than just feeling like my own thoughts, which was great, ’cause I needed that. A lot of this is about belief, and you have to, like Wily Coyote, you have to walk out on the edge and just like, “Ah, I believe I can move the tinfoil.” And you have to really sit with that for a long time. Like, “No, I know I can, I know I can.” And then eventually, one day it happens, and you’re like, “Holy crap, I knew.” We’ll get to that in the poem at the end.

It’s happened to me so many times now. But I still do it. I still, like, “I don’t know. Maybe I’m making it up. Maybe that didn’t really happen.” Like, “I’m just gonna try to hold this belief. I’m just gonna try to hold it. It’s gonna happen. This is how it works, I know.” And then you go, it sounds easy, but… And then eventually it happens, so you’re like, “Holy crap. Okay. All right, cool.”

And for those wondering, I’ve asked for real world validation. I get definitions to terms I’ve never heard of before. I will lose stuff around the house and just randomly go, “Ugh.” It’s like somebody’s frustrated with me. Like, “It’s in the bottom drawer near the sink.” I’m like, “That’s not where I put that.” Then I’ll look everywhere else around the house, like there’s no way. I go, and I’m like, “Fine.” I go look in the bottom drawer near the sink, and there it is, and there’s just this sense of laughter. And it’s like, “Man, goddammit.”

Joshua: That would be helpful. I need that. Having two young kids, your mind is just like a chaos.

Steven: Yeah. But they’re not always there. I don’t know. It’s just like, just checking in on you. “Hey, what’s up?” Or they could always be there, right? There’s not always presence.

Joshua: How does that shift your sense of self and autonomy, knowing that you’re always being watched essentially, right?

Steven: Well, surprise, everyone’s always being watched. Everyone’s got a loved one or a guide or someone.

Joshua: We don’t realize that we are, right? But now you know.

Grandmas

Steven: Yeah. Time is a thing here. Beyond the 3D material, linear space-time world, there’s no scarcity of time, and if somebody cares about you, their grandma, whatever, they’re probably watching all the time.

I got a crazy medium story where after the mindsight training, some of the ladies there were mediums. And they’re training, right? There’s five old ladies. They bring me into a mediumship session. I’ve never done this before, not like this. Like, cool, I’ll just sit there. So they stare at me for 20 minutes over Zoom. And then they take turns reading impressions of whoever showed up, like deceased loved ones.

And it’s interesting, I have this experience of, well, that was cool and all. It’s kinda hard to place some of this stuff. Like, eh, that was neat, I guess. No dead ringers or anything. But I just have this sense of sadness that I didn’t expect, and I realized I kinda just wanted them to tell me that they loved me. And instead I got a lot of, “This is about my life. Here’s some advice,” a little bit of lecturing. Okay, cool, but that’s all I really wanted was that you think about me and you care about me. I didn’t know to ask for that. There’s nobody else’s fault.

And so after that happened, I didn’t expect to feel those emotions. I didn’t really share that with anybody. It was just a private internal thing, like, “Oh man, I feel a little hurt, actually.” I didn’t think I would.

And so the next morning, I get a text message from somebody who was in that mindsight training, and she messages me and she says, “I was thinking about you when I was making my coffee this morning,” ’cause we shared the same living space while we were training. And she’s like, “I also had this really weird feeling that I needed to reach out to you, something about grandmas and loving you. Does that mean anything to you? I’m sorry if it doesn’t because I know that’s totally out of the blue. We haven’t talked in a long time. I just felt like I had to tell you that for some reason.”

And I’m like, “Oh my God, grandmas.” So I mean, if you buy the story, I mean, that happened. Some grandma is like, “Oh no, this boy needs to know I love him.” Like, hold up. Go grab the nearest person you can possibly—

Joshua: I had so much to say, I forgot to say the most important thing.

Steven: Well, I think it’s more like, “Oh, you don’t think that we— You didn’t know that? Well, you need to know that.” No, this is much more like, “I’m gonna make sure you know we love you.” If that’s not such a grandma thing. But I get validation like this all the time. I don’t get a lot of it because I think it’s counterproductive. You’re supposed to start trusting your non-primary physical senses more.

But yeah, all this stuff is what happens to me at this stage, and I guess we can round it out with where I’m at now, if that’s okay with you.

The gift of everything

Joshua: This feels like a good place to begin landing. I’m curious to hear, having shared all of that and gone through this process of reflecting, what meaning do you make of your journey?

Steven: Well, I never expected this. What a plot twist of a life. I like to think of my life as a story now at this point. A co-created story, but less attached to events. I think that’s a better way of looking at it. I realized the power of intention and that we can kind of have almost any experience we want within reason. We’re in a place where there are rules and order and physics, and that’s good. That’s like a different thing. You’re not gonna be able to snap your fingers and make a giant block of gold appear. Well, I mean, probably not. And that’s good because it would defeat the purpose of a lot of the reason we’re here.

And so I think a stage of development I’m in now is I have everything I want in life for the most part. I’m happy, I’m healthy, I’m stable, and that’s really when this started to pick up. And now I have a sense of why am I here? Both me authoring why I’m here, but also being receptive to more of being used like a tool, like in a sort of pre-agreement. You could call that a calling or a purpose. They actually kind of line up, and I feel like intuitively, much more directly, like literally just being told sometimes, “Why are you here?” But it’s more like a menu, like it’s an offer. “Here’s what you’re good at, here’s what the world needs. Do this.” But mostly it’s an intuitive sense.

So I’m moving into a stage where I have a feeling like I know what I want out of life, and now I’m more interested in helping. What do you do with a good thing? All of this is awesome. A surprise, for those who are unaware, you’re not gonna die when you’re dead. You get to live forever, and reality is way, way better than you thought it was, and you can have almost anything you want. So there’s the gift.

In fact, one of these journals is me waking up in the middle of the night, somebody very surprised, like a surprise party and just like, “What is this thing?” Telling me happy birthday. I think, what are you talking about? It’s like, “You’re about to get a gift.” And then it’s like, “What’s the gift?” “It’s the gift of everything. You’re about to learn that it’s all awesome, and you can have almost anything you want, and you’re not gonna die.” Like, “Oh, cool.” But now I know it, right? I know it on a deeper level.

So life is more playful and more, what can I do with this, right? How can I have fun? How can I live into this? How can I do something meaningful? And that’s sort of the stage I’m in. I’m in the rarefied position of, from my perspective, kinda understanding the 80/20 of how reality and consciousness works and who I am and how it all works, the big questions. I wrote yesterday in a journal that I feel like I have satisfactory and firsthand validated answers to all the big questions I’ve ever come up with. That is a very strange position to be in as a human being. Well, now that those boxes are checked, I guess I’m just gonna have fun and try to help other people have fun too. So that’s where I’m at. I recommend it.

Joshua: I really like that you said reality is way better than you think it is. I love that.

Steven: And it keeps happening.

Just keep going

Joshua: And so much of your story just testifies to that. If anyone listening is moving through their own rupture, what would you like them to hear?

Steven: I would say that your thoughts, emotions, feelings, beliefs, they are not you, so chill out. They’re part of an experience, but they are also what’s controlling what you get to a large degree eventually, right? It’s imagination, all of these things that I treated as secondary or ephemeral or just like icing on a reality cake, turns out they are what determines the cake, like eventually. Think of it as like steering something very slowly, but sometimes not so slowly. And the more you can lean into that, the more you’ll get what you want.

You’re always creating your own reality to a degree or largely influencing here, eventually creating whole cloth. And the good news is you have inherited a unconditionally loving and completely connected universe that wants nothing but to give you everything you want and cares about you and fundamentally, like no conditions. It didn’t have to be this good. It doesn’t always feel that way from our current seat, but we’re playing on hard mode right now. And don’t worry so much because the big things are already sorted out. You already inherited everything you could ever want, and even if you screw up here completely, it doesn’t really matter. You’re already good forever. So don’t worry too much.

And if that doesn’t feel true yet, the advice I keep getting over and over again every time I hit a moment where it’s like, “Holy crap, what is reality? How do I go to work tomorrow? I can see through my eyelids, and I’m talking to people in my head that appear to be real. I don’t know. How do I keep going? How do I integrate this? How do I make sense of anything? What is even real anymore?” The answer is just keep going.

If you can do that, if you can somehow get yourself to a positive orientation and just keep going, you’ll make it. You’ll find your own experiences. You have your own revelations. It won’t be the same as mine. We’re all different, and so you get your own personalized experience, and it’ll keep happening. The magic will be in the world as long as you expect it to be there and as long as you keep playing. So hopefully that’s helpful. Just don’t stop playing.

Joshua: Don’t stop playing. I dig it. Do you have anywhere people can find out about your work or your writing, your thinking?

Steven: Yeah. I write fiction, but it’s not super relevant to this. But I’ve been trying to write about my experiences or distilling, I think, important lessons on a blog. I don’t get anything out of this. It’s just read it. I don’t need anything. I’m in the wonderful position of I’ve got what I need. I just wanna have good times with good people. So by all means, if you found this useful, please go read that. But you kinda already have everything you need is the advice I give to everyone else. But if I can help you make sense of it or Joshua can, by all means, borrow and cheat and steal. I’ve cheated every opportunity I get. Why wait in line?

To Know

Joshua: Thank you, Steven. As we’re closing, I’m just feeling gratitude and awe in hearing your story and reinvigorated on my own path and practice, and so many of the things you’ve brought into the circle that we’ve initiated together and in our conversations have motivated me to try new things and go in new paths. And every time I close my eyes now, I tell myself, “I can see without my eyes,” and even at the end of my meditation, I’ve been trying to affirm that, remember to affirm that.

Steven: Heck yeah.

Joshua: So I’m excited to know you and be inspired on the path that you’re treading for your own life. In a couple words, how are you checking out today?

Steven: I’m just grateful to have fellow people to be on the journey with, you know? Inspired is exactly what I hope for because I kinda just want other people to go get their own version of this. It’s waiting for them. You don’t have to live in mundane reality anymore. If you just ask, just say, “Hey, I want my version of this,” and then take whatever breadcrumb comes your way, and something will, and just keep following those. The universe will play with you, however you wanna frame it. It’ll give you the next step. Keep following those steps, and you can have your own version of this, and I hope that everyone does that, you included. ‘Cause I wanna live in that world with other people. Who wants to do it alone? That’s no fun.

Joshua: Let’s all become yogis with yogic superpowers, flying around, communicating with our mind and build a world like that.

Steven: Yeah, whatever your version is. Whatever you want.

Joshua: That’s a fun world I wanna live in. Thank you, Steven. We always like to close with a little offering from the guest, so if you have something, please share it with us.

Steven: Cool. I did select something. I wrote a poem. I don’t usually like poetry because it makes me do too much work, and that stresses me out. Did I understand it correctly? I don’t know. So I don’t actually enjoy it because I’m too psyched out about it. But I decided to write one, and this happened after one of many things that I never thought could actually be true ended up being true. So that’s the context to know. So the poem itself is called “To Know.”

To know
That you really are the person you hoped you’d be
That the things you told yourself you didn’t need
Were fundamental truths from a changed heart
And not the lamentations of a spurned one
When this life offers you opportunity to prove
You will feel that solitary triumph
No one knows of the tension held so long, even you forgot it was there
It was true
Oh, it was true all along
This is what it feels like to know
They were not just words

Reality 101

Episode 1

Jeff Cameron · before you read

This is the night the series is named for. He dates it to the end of December, five months before this recording. He wakes in the state between sleeping and waking, and what follows comes in two movements: a meeting, first with a woman he calls his soul twin and then with a presence he calls Mother, and a lesson.

Two things to notice. One is how ordinary the first half is. Someone offers him cake and gets impatient with him. The other is his reaction to being shown, as he understands it, how everything works. He is disappointed. Then he makes a choice, and I think that choice is the hinge of the whole series.

Steven: Reality 101 is a journal of impossible experiences, from first-hand explorations into the nature of reality, what it means for all of us, and how you can experience it yourself. I’m Steven Schrembeck.

Joshua: I’m Joshua Swenson, and today we’ll be exploring Reality 101. This is both the name of the series and the starting point, an experience where Steven was lectured on how it all works on a very basic level when consciousness is the foundation of reality. But before we get into that, I just wanted to check in with you, Steven. How are you arriving today?

Steven: I’m arriving pretty darn good. Had a great meditation this morning. It’s a weird one. A lot of them are weird ones these days. We’ll get into some of that later. But I’m coming in feeling pretty playful. I’m really excited to start sharing these stories. Like I’ve told you, I’ve felt like I’ve been walking around with a golden tome, a couple tomes at this point. I just wanna get it out, so I feel like this is a form of catharsis for me.

Joshua: I’m excited about this too. I haven’t done collaboration like this before, so I feel like it’s a really exciting time, stepping into this. And the other thing I wanted to check in about is just that since I’ve met you and since I’ve been doing some of the practices that you’ve introduced me to, some Mindsight training, some psi wheel spinning, sticking coins to walls, I’ve just been feeling like I’m stepping into such a strange world. I’ve always hoped and believed that this stuff was real. I studied Buddhism many years ago, and Hinduism, and yoga, and I was always fascinated with the yogic superpowers, the siddhis, and I think that’s maybe part of why I wanted to meditate.

Steven: You’re a wizard, Joshua.

Joshua: I’ve been meditating for 20 years because I’ve wanted to float. And now it’s like, holy shit, are we really in the Matrix?

Steven: It’s better than that.

Joshua: I’ve been having some really strange dreams, which I know you’ve talked about as you were stepping into some of the stuff. A little unsettling, and I feel like I’m approaching this big threshold crossing. I’ve been across many thresholds in my own journey of awakening and self-transformation. And yeah, this is a big one. So our mutual friend Kate and I are talking to Steven’s other friend, Miguel, about hosting a private Mindsight session.

Steven: That is seeing without eyeballs.

Joshua: I’m looking forward to that. Yes, seeing without eyeballs. Kate had some incredible breakthroughs when we did a little training the other day, and so it’s just exciting. It’s exciting to see people I know making these breakthroughs and to just be witnessing you and your journey. And that being said, I’m curious now to dive in on this experience, Reality 101. How did this begin for you?

Picking up the call

Steven: Now, before I get into it, I’m glad that you mention that because this is real. The things I’m about to say are stories, and they’re profound, and they’re exciting and hopefully entertaining and captivating. It was for me, but this happened to me. And it is now happening to you, some version of this, and that is the whole purpose of this series, is sharing this so that people can understand their own reality and make sense of it, but also learn how to do it yourselves.

So, without further ado, let’s pick up where we left off after the prior episode.

Joshua: We’ll just say that we recorded a prior conversation on my other podcast, Ruptures and Roots, where Steven talks about his backstory and what led him into this journey.

Steven: Yeah, you need that context. So at this point, what I’m gonna talk about is one of the most insane and profound things that had ever happened to me. Even now, I’ve experienced things like it since then. But it really does have to be described. To put it on an index card, one evening five months ago, I was shown how reality works at a very low level in a lecture-type setting, by somebody who is not physical and walked me through the 101 of how consciousness and matter and everything exists, how it all works, where it came from more or less as far as we’re related, how this is happening.

And I was able to ask any questions I wanted, and I got all the answers that I asked. So that’s what we’re gonna get into. So picking it up from there, I go to sleep the night prior, and I knew that I was going to talk to somebody. I had had the intuition, but at this point, whatever, right? I go to sleep. I wake up, and where we left off from our prior interview is I wake up in a state where I am laying in my bed.

I am awake. Call these liminal states, just falling asleep or waking up. Part of you is awake, so I can get up out of bed. I’m conscious. But part of you is still not quite fully self-conscious. You’re not attached to the world yet, right? Kinda like a daydream. And I get a little nudge from someone familiar. At this point in my story, telepathy is a thing that happens. But it’s messy, right? 80% of the time I can’t tell whether they’re my thoughts or not. It’s pretty frustrating, honestly. And sometimes it’s very clear.

I get a little nudge, almost like your phone ringing. Imagine it’s like somebody’s waiting there, they wanna talk to you. You just know that they’re waiting there. So I pick up the call. Normally this is like verbal thoughts. We do a little back and forth, “Hey, how are you?” It feels a little like talking to yourself and you kind of humor it. I pick up the call. It is a very much different call this time.

I get visuals immediately. It is like somebody is writing into your imagination. So imagine seeing something like an apple on a table. Now imagine an apple on the table appears in your visual imagination right now, and you are not making it happen. You’re not trying to make it happen. You’re not doing anything, and it’s stable, and it’s like somebody’s playing a movie. You’re the projector and they’re shining a movie onto you. And there’s a person there, and she’s a girl, and she is, as I described in our prior interview, like my twin, my soul twin sister.

And she’s always existed. I knew who she was. She’s familiar. I had been getting thoughts and feelings from this person for months, and I’d been denying it for a long time. At this point I was on board, but I never thought I would actually meet them or see them. And so my eyes are closed and I’m trying not to focus on my body because I feel it’s pulling me out of the experience. And so I just let it happen. I let the tape roll.

I start to work through it a little bit. But it’s stressful and wonderful and exciting, and I am talking to somebody. It’s in a white space, actually a lot like The Matrix. It’s just a white plane with a ground. There’s nothing else. Stuff just pops in while we’re talking. Music just starts playing. Sound effects start happening that are always related to what we’re talking about. It’s like you just pull in these ideas and they just start happening. It was incredibly profound, but I’ve covered a lot of that already.

You can ask any questions you want about that, ’cause we didn’t go into a ton of detail. But we’re getting into Reality 101.

My soul twin

Joshua: That’s a good place to pause. I have a couple questions. I’m curious, what did she look like? How did your soul twin appear to you in that space?

Steven: Well, she looks a lot like me. That’s probably not surprising. Same sort of body type. We look genetically related. Sort of dirty blonde hair. About 30 years old, I’d guess. Wearing nondescript clothes. She’s pretty kind. I know who she is. You can feel like there’s more than just verbal thoughts happening. You can feel emotions and their ideas or their intent. If somebody said a word that had two definitions and they just said that, you would know which one they meant, even without the rest of the context. You just understand what they’re trying to communicate to you. But we are using words.

But yeah, she’s just standing there, and she looks worried and hopeful. I’ve taken journal notes, like 200 something experiences at this point. I wrote in my notes that she is keen. She’s watching me, incredibly observant. Like this whole thing has been a setup for a long time, and this person has been observing me. And is just watching like a hawk for every little reaction, every little tell that I have.

She wants to know how much do I understand about what’s happening, about who this is. Where am I on a stage of development, basically, right? That’s what it feels like. But also, “Do you miss me? How much do you remember about who I am? What can you feel? Do you care? Are you blowing this off? Does this really matter to you? Do you wanna know me?” Imagine seeing your brother after 36 years, and you haven’t seen them in that long, and they don’t remember you, but you know everything about them, and how that might feel.

And it was almost like somebody at a military coming home party or something after a year or two gone, and they’re really nervous but excited. That’s what it was like.

Joshua: And how did you respond to her presence?

Steven: All right. Well, I think this deserves a story. Rather than just tell you, here, I’ll look through my actual notes, because I wrote it down. So some of this I’ll just read it ’cause it will be easier. So she is painfully sincere. Who you see is largely who she is. It’s incredibly refreshing. There’s weird artifacts in the communication format, but it’s incredibly clear, right? And she’s incredibly observant and absorbing every detail of our interaction.

We have this initial conversation about, “Hey, don’t freak out. You’re gonna break the contact. You’re gonna break the communication.” Sort of stabilize it. We just do some basic chitchat, but I’m basically staring like a slack jaw. “Holy shit, you are real.” I thought maybe that was the case. But I’m seeing you, and you have facial expressions, and you are standing there, and you clearly want things out of me.

I look like an idiot, dude. I’m totally slack jawed. I’m stopping mid-sentence and just staring ’cause I’m waiting for this to be fake. I feel like it’s just going to stop at any time or just be my imagination, but it’s so stable, and there’s a person there, and she’s getting impatient.

The cake

Steven: After all that, the first thing she does is offer me a cake. In fact, she just says, “Do you want cake?” That’s the opening line. And I’m just like, “Cake?” And then she just looks at me like I’m a complete effing idiot. Lovingly, ’cause I’m in shock, basically. Probably literally in shock. She says, “I made cake.” That’s it. And I’m just staring. It’s got a plate with a cake on it. It’s just a brown, unfrosted cake. And I’m staring at it, and I say, “What is it?” “It’s cake,” is the response. I’m looking at my notes. I’m like, “How did you make it?”

Sort of an eye roll, like, “I baked it in an oven.” And then we teleport to a convection oven, and it’s just sitting there on the white plain. She opens it up, like, “See? Oven.” But there’s this undertone of, “Come on, dude. Keep up. It’s an oven. I made a cake in an oven. This is not the hard part. Let’s move on.” This is the tone of what’s happening. And I’m like, “Okay.” And so I’m like, “Sure.” I don’t know what to do.

I don’t know how to interact. I’ve been fighting for scraps of words for months, and half the time or more, it’s my own thoughts, and it goes poorly. It’s not fun. Telepathy hadn’t been fun up until this point, but now it’s like, whoa, I’m watching TV, man. Well, it’s interactive. It’s like they’re standing in front of me. And so she gives me cake and a knife and I don’t really have a body. I’m just floating. I don’t know how to navigate these spaces at this point in time. This is five months ago. I knew a lot less. Things have happened since then.

But we’re sizing up a slice, right? And then as I’m going to cut it, she says, “That’s too big.” I just start laughing. And I’m like, “Okay.” So I do slightly smaller slice. I get a nod and a literal beamed sense of approval. Like, good. Okay. Yes, that’s appropriate. And I eat it. You know what it tastes like?

Joshua: Cake.

Steven: Just tastes like cake. So I asked if it was vegan. Okay, I wrote, “She informs me patiently, as if talking to a small child, that there are no chickens here to make eggs, so it’s fine.” And I just start laughing. I’m like, “Okay, yeah, that was a stupid question.” It’s like we’re in an imaginary space. Yeah, we’re good. Never mind. And then I say there’s no frosting. There’s this whole coded interaction, right?

This is very clearly, we’re not talking about the thing that we both wanna talk about, like, what the hell is happening to you right now? Or, we finally get to see each other. We’re not talking about the thing. You can imagine the social tension here. That’s what’s happening. I will also add that she gets her own slice of cake. The cake transforms into a heart-shaped cake immediately after that. No words are spoken.

She’s totally neutral face. Straight line face emoji. Just playing it very cool, like I’m a wild animal or something, and I could just flip out and bite her or just run away at any time. But played totally cool. Cake transforms into a heart-shaped cake. Otherwise, it’s the same. Then she cuts her own slice, and it is enormous. I just start cracking up. I’m like, “What the hell?” I’m like, “All right. I see how this is going. I’m getting played. I gotta keep up. I forgot you’re my twin. Hold on a second.” So this is what the interactions are like. A lot of it is just social stuff.

Joshua: You didn’t come back with, “That’s too big”?

Steven: No, I didn’t. I asked, “How did you learn how to make cake?” She waves at just the white space around her and says, “It’s all out there, and then practice.” I’m like, “How many have you made before?” The answers are immediately. She’s just talking to me. That alone is what’s blowing my mind right now, is that any of this is happening. And so we get into a bunch of other stuff from there. I don’t know how much is relevant, because we haven’t even gotten to the main experience.

But this is profound for me, because this is my sister. I know who she is. It’s difficult to describe how it feels to know how somebody is but not remember them. I know who they are. It’s like I’ve always known them.

Just people

Joshua: You’d had impressions of this person before too, right?

Steven: Yeah, for many months up until this. And this was the person that I’d been talking to the most. Not the only one. There was another person. We’ll introduce them shortly. But yes, my sister. I had been doing remote viewing. I had been doing so much, and I just started getting verbal thoughts and out of context emotions and other intuitive senses. You imagine just beaming stuff at you.

Telepathy is basically a shared experience. So if I’m jumping around feeling happy and thinking about, I don’t know, a concert I’m gonna go to, another person’s experience of telepathy receiving that is that they are jumping around happy and excited about a concert. But it’s a little bit distant. It’s removed. You can feel both at the same time. Imagine a kid who comes up to you, and they’re super excited about a toy they just got. You can’t help but be excited in the same way.

It’s like that, but you can get more than just emotions. You can get somatic senses. You can get ideas. They bleed through, but they have a distance to them. You can feel that they aren’t yours. You feel yours separately. So I’d been getting a lot of impressions all over the place, right? So there’s a lot of lead-up to this.

This is the big break where, oh, we are interacting real time in high fidelity with no error. I’m not guessing whether these are my thoughts or imagination. I’m not pushing this away and saying, “It isn’t real.” You’re here. I’m here. And I’m awake, right? I’m experiencing it not as 100% fidelity as seeing the room around you, but it’s 70% of that. It’s stable and sharp. I can see, I can touch, I can smell, I can taste. So that’s where I’m at at this point in time. And it’s wild.

But I’m blown away by her, right? It’s weird how familiar… They’re just people. Non-physical people are just people. They’re different, and we’ll have a lot of time to get into that in this series, but they’re just people, ultimately. And everybody has a family, whether it’s deceased loved ones or a soul family like this. But they’re all just people who love you or care about you and have their own ideas and motivations and problems. And so a lot of what our interactions are about is just a person, family, social stuff. What are you into? How do you feel? So that’s what’s going on.

Joshua: What’s her name?

Steven: Names are very, very hard. You wouldn’t think that they would be, but I think that there’s a lot of reasons for it. Maybe we’ll get into it another time. But for people who start to have telepathic experiences, which everyone has, whether you realize it or not, but directed, like conversations, not just perceiving random stuff from the ether, names are hard. I didn’t get a name until much, much later. She does have a name. I don’t think I’m gonna share it because I’m pretty sure that people could attempt to contact her with it.

But she goes by Iris. That is a given name. So we picked that one pretty early on, long before this experience, because I needed something. What am I gonna call you? Who are you? I’d already figured out who she was to me at that point, but I didn’t know… I needed a name. So that’s the name we went with, and it stuck. So that’s what she goes by.

Joshua: And that makes me think of how we have strong associations with our own name because from the moment we come into this world, people are repeating these syllables, directing their attention at us. And at some point, we begin to associate ourselves with that series of syllables, but it’s not really who we are. So in a way, it becomes a kind of prison. I’ve experienced that. I used to go by Josh. And at some point, I felt that Josh was not who I was anymore. And I know people have life-changing experiences, and they might pick a totally new name. I just decided to start going by Joshua because it was easier.

Steven: It’s an easy transition. That’s good. I didn’t know that about you. Thank you. But that’s a perfect example. There’s more than that, too. I think this is a way of framing it, if you’re gonna go down this road, it’s best just to treat these experiences like there’s another person on the other end of the line. Many of them have names. A lot of them don’t. And I think it’s because they go by the feeling of their own soul.

You can feel who they are, and that’s kind of your name, right? That’s the hallmark. And it’s always representative of you, so there’s no real reason to… Many of them don’t have names. Some of them do, and if you ask for a name, you’ll get a bogus one, and it’s hilarious every time. It’s great. We’ll get some very interesting names later on in this series. But it’s like, okay, yeah, that’s totally your name. All right, let’s continue. You need something to attach them to.

An idea space

Steven: But anyway, this experience is with Iris, and then this night is wild. All kinds of things are happening to me while this is happening. It’s a stable experience, but I don’t know where we are. I just call it an astral plane or something. I don’t know whether that’s the right term or not. Coming into this, I had not done a lot of study on esoteric stuff, traditions. I don’t know the words for these. I’m just experiencing it. So we’re in a place where stuff just happens.

I’m seeing things, like pictures, images, sounds pop in that are tangentially related to what we’re doing and sometimes not. Or I feel like my body spazzing out, like I’m being electrocuted, but not in a bad way. Just this ton of something, energy going through me or something. We’ll mention, I don’t know, tennis or something, and then you just hear the sounds of people playing tennis or something, or music will start playing.

Or I start hearing her thoughts spoken aloud. Her own thoughts are being verbalized, loud, like inner thoughts, ’cause you have those separately. And she just says, “Yeah, that happens sometimes. Stuff just bleeds in.” And I’m like, “Okay. I’ll just pretend like that’s fine.” But it’s so matter-of-fact. She’s just like, “Whatever. Stop paying attention. This is not the important part.” Like somebody who’s been living there for I don’t know how long, this is just normal for them.

It’s an idea space. Things just pop up. You don’t walk. You just teleport to where you’re going. You wanna show me an oven? Pop. Here it is. There’s the oven. You don’t need to make an oven. You don’t need to buy one. It’s right here. You need an oven? It’s right here. When you don’t need it, it’s gone. You could just tell by the way she acted that that is what the reality is like, is it’s imagination. Literally, it’s imagination. But not because it’s made up, but because that’s just how it is, and humanity and physical dwelling beings are a bit oddballs out.

Joshua: I was just writing, imagination is a real place. That is a pretty profound statement coming from our modern Western culture. Older traditions would say the imaginal is what would lend more credence to spaces like that, and that the imagination is source in a way. It’s the source of this world or one of the sources, maybe the loading zone. Whereas I’d always, maybe up until a certain number of years in my own creative journey, thought of imagination as just fancy, just happening in your own head.

I keep thinking of the Matrix loading space, the white space where Neo’s like, “Guns,” and then a bunch of guns come. They get anything they need before they plug in and come into physical reality, right? And I’m also curious if you could say something about how you have come to experience this liminal state as valid. Because what you were describing earlier is an interesting experience, and if I just heard anybody else talk about that, I’d be like, “Oh, that’s an interesting dream. You met a lady and she offered you some cake.” And it’s like, what is that saying about your psyche, or how is this just in your own head? But what you’re expressing is that this was an actual experience with another non-physical being.

Doubt is baked in

Steven: Great points. First of all, doubt is so easy. It is just baked into the human psyche. Even after this set of experiences, I still experienced doubt. But once you see it that way, you just start laughing. You’re like, “Okay. Yeah, buddy. Yeah, there’s totally nothing happening.” But doubt is baked in, and it’s one of the things that kills these experiences fully, right? I think I wrote once in one of my articles on my blog that you need to be able to entertain a stupid idea long enough to see if it’s true.

And the real answer is that I’d been receiving intrusive communication for five, six months before this, out of nowhere. Deep in meditation, somebody’s talking to me. I’m not making these thoughts happen. I don’t know where they’re coming from. This has never happened to me before. I was 36 years old. It’s a little late to have a schizophrenic episode. It doesn’t make sense. I was healthy and happy, and my life had been better than ever.

And in fact, the communication, any messaging at all, goes away when I got stressed or I didn’t have sleep. So it would turn off. You would think it would be the opposite. A dysfunctional brain is gonna start acting up. No, I couldn’t get anything. It was clearly just me. And I could only really start to get this when I was healthy and free and clear and open and playful. That’s when it started to come through, like somebody is talking to me.

But only in these sort of more enlightened or happy, clear, permissive states. And that’s why it happens so often in the liminal states or deep in meditation, is because your conscious mind is out of the way. You’re not blocking it. We have filters up, and I had been working at this point to start tearing down the filters because I was long past the point of accepting that there is somebody there. I know a lot of this is noise, a lot of this is confusing, but something is happening. I’m talking to somebody. I’m feeling their emotions.

I’m talking to my kid, and then I get this sudden feeling of affection or curiosity as to what they’re doing, and it’s not mine. I’m like, “Pssh. Go away, kid. I wanna do something else.” And almost like, I don’t know, a family member that is riding around in my experience or attached to it, non-physical, and I am feeling what they’re feeling, which is, “Oh, it’s your son. What does he want? That’s so cool. He looks just like you.” This stuff happens to me. It’s not all words, and it’s out of nowhere.

And these people have personalities. They will say a thing, have a conversation. Imagine entertaining what feels like an imaginary conversation in your hammock one day and you’re like, “Ah, did any of that happen?” It felt like it did. I felt like I was talking to someone. And then two weeks later, they bring up a thing that you totally forgot about and have an answer to, and they’ve been working on it, or they’ve made some change in the way they try to talk to you. You didn’t even notice until you remember, “Oh, didn’t I say something about that two weeks ago?” I haven’t even been thinking about this.

Now, is any of this impossible for the human mind? No. The human mind is an incredible thing. But all of these things add up to people who have memories and motives and desires of their own, and I can feel them. That is really what pushes me over the edge to taking this seriously, to get to a point where I can even have an experience like this, because you have to entertain it a long time, is that I can feel their emotions.

And that’s really what kept me going, is that somebody shows up, just a smile in your head, like, “Hey, how are you doing today?” You’re like, “Oh, the signal’s clear.” Like, “What’s up? How are you doing?” And I’m like, “Shut up. This isn’t real.” I feel this intense betrayal, pain of rejection. “F you.” Man, they don’t even need to say that. You can just feel it. It’s like a heartache. You know what the feeling of your heart literally hurting feels like.

That would hit me, and it was so out of context with what I was feeling. I was doing the opposite. I’m like, “Go away. This is dumb. I’m dismissing this. Go away.” And then I feel this intense pain of rejection. And you can only do that so many times before you’re like, “All right. Let’s see what’s here.” And then the pattern becomes a person, and then I experience them like this. So it was a long lead-up to this moment.

It was more relief than any… Like I mentioned in our first interview, I would’ve loved to have been crazy. I sought every kind of help that I could think of. I was no stranger to my own mind and to therapy techniques, and I had no shame in any of this. I sought every kind of help or diagnosis I could reasonably seek. I was fine. I was better than I’d ever been. So it was completely out of context with any mental disorder. It made no sense. And so I just decided, well, reality is weirder than we thought it was. Let’s see where this goes. I don’t wanna keep hurting these people.

Joshua: Reality is weirder and wilder and far more exciting than anything we’ve been led to believe. I’m gradually coming to accept that as well, so I also really appreciate naming the doubt and hearing about your journey of doubting these experiences, ’cause we’re diving in several years in. You’ve been on this journey for several years. So I think that’s helpful for me and probably for anyone else listening, to see that you started from a place of doubt and skepticism and pushing this away.

And I also just, my heart goes out to this non-physical person trying to reach you. I just was imagining, I have two young children, and imagine if I’m trying to help them and guide them and they just don’t believe I’m real. They can’t see me or feel me. I love them so much and I care for them and I wanna see them grow. So yeah. That’s really beautiful. So let’s get into the Reality 101 piece. What was this lecture that you received?

Family beyond our family

Steven: We’ll talk a lot more about imagination later. But for now, just think of imagination as being like a real space that you’re a participant of one. It’s yours. It’s your personal space. But it turns out that other people can come into your personal space. It’s like your room. They can show up if you let them, or you have the capacity to recognize it. And that is what starts happening.

So I’m talking to these people, and this stuff is hitting me, right? I’m experiencing an enormous amount of emotions and profound realizations. I’m not just chit-chatting with a long-lost sister. I’m like, “You’re not my biological sister. You’re my soul sister.” So I’d been wondering for a while, okay, if there are lives beyond lives, do we have family beyond our family here? And well, here’s my confirmation. Yes, we do. I mean, some of us do. I think many of us do. I couldn’t tell you for sure. This is a sample size of one.

But I’m starting to wonder aloud. I’m thinking in my head, personal thoughts. I’m not projecting out. Just thinking, wait, so if I have a sister, do we have parents? How did we come to exist? Hold on. I’m experiencing all of this, right? I’m two hours in maybe at this point, three hours in. I’m just laying there. My brain’s starting to cook, right? Whoof, there’s a lot going on here.

But I need to know. I’ve had scraps of information for so long, of insights, stuff through remote viewing. And this is just, bam, full fidelity, ask whatever you want. It’s right here. Drinking from the fire hydrant. I gotta get as much as I can while it’s clear and obvious and there’s no signal, like, “Was that my own thoughts?”

And so I’m thinking this to myself, and she’s like, “Oh yeah, pshh.” And she starts to pull in my grandmother, my deceased grandmother, into the space, and I freak out. I’m like, “Nope.” I reject it immediately to the point where the whole thing is starting to tear apart here. And I’m just like, “Nope, okay, that doesn’t happen.” I am not ready for that. For whatever reason, deceased family members is a line for me. New people that I don’t have memories of but feel familiar, that’s okay. For whatever reason, that’s easier. And I’m like, “Nope, I can’t do that.”

But then I start to wonder, do we have a parent? Where the heck did we come from? And she’s like, “Oh yeah, pshh.” This exact quote, “Oh yeah, pshh, that’s easy. She’s super easy to talk to. Here.” That’s just a hand wave, so nonchalant. The whole thing is like, yeah, whatever. This is just how I live. Here, talk to her. I’m like, “Wah.” I could just barely handle talking to you and realizing you were real, and we’ve had five to six months of warm-up here.

And sure enough, immediately, I’m gone. I’m out of that place. In an instant. She’s just hand wave. Sure, yeah. You can talk to her. I’m now in a black space. There’s nothing. It’s just empty space without stars, basically. I still don’t have a body. I’m floating out there. And I feel this smile, this presence. Nobody. I think she knew then that I was not ready to see another person. I was really at the breaking point here.

I could not handle a lot of trauma. It’s the personal stuff that really gets to me, even more than just the profound and weird stuff. I’m not ready to see you yet, as silly as that sounds. But I could feel her, and she was super zen, really loving, just the perfect portrait of a parent who’s incredibly loving and is very patient and familiar. I knew exactly who this was. This was not only familiar in the sense that, oh, I haven’t seen you in a long time, but I felt this throughout my life.

And of course I have, because she’s basically been there the whole time. Just quietly in the background. And I think that you would call this role a spirit guide. This is the role that she’s been acting out. I think we all have a lot of them, but this is mine. My main one. And of course it would be, it’s my mom. My soul mom. I think at one point she even told me, this is outside this experience, “Why would I let anyone else do this job?” Obviously. You’re my kid. I’m doing this personally.

And so I can feel them, and it’s incredibly surreal. And I’m brought to tears by just the very presence of this person, and I can tell how much it means to her to be seen. Imagine what it’s like floating around your kid for 36 years and they don’t know that you’re real or that you even exist, and then they finally notice you. How that must feel. So I’m feeling all that, too, at the same time. This is what I’m feeling. So that’s a lot of emotion, and I’m really feeling it.

Mother is good enough

Steven: So I just start asking questions. I just jump right into it, right? “Hello. What do I call you?” “Mother is good enough,” right? Again, these names. You like this. It’s like, okay, here’s your label. Let’s move on. And I realize that this is a conversation that I was going to get weeks before. And it was just verbal thoughts. Waking up in the middle of the night and just starting to tell me stuff.

And I’m like, “Eh, I can tell that you’re important and I feel I know who you are, but wait until we can meet better. I don’t want it like this. I don’t want an audio message. I wanna meet you.” And this is that. And this had been set up. This whole thing had been set up. It was clearly orchestrated for me to meet my family beyond my family, right?

Cool, you’re ready now. You’re receptive enough. You’ve been doing the practice, you’ve been doing the meditation, you’ve been doing the psychic stuff. You’re open. You’re taking it seriously. Cool. We’re gonna engineer this experience for you. We’re not gonna abduct you. You’re not gonna go out of body. We’re gonna make it as gentle as possible. Here we go. Nice and gentle.

And so I start asking questions. “Okay, who are you?” And she starts talking. She tells me that she created our souls and another sibling, who’s not our twin. And it’s very emotional. So she’s incredibly familiar to me and very unbothered by all this stuff. I’m cracking jokes or whatever, and she’s like a monk. Nothing I said could have made her upset.

I think, honestly, not that she couldn’t be upset, but I don’t know how to describe it. Older souls, how do you wanna frame this? They are not bothered by literally anything that a child could do. You could stick up your middle finger and moon them and they would just smile at you. It’s like that.

So I get hugged. I get the sense of a hug, the somatic sense of being hugged. And then I’m reassured about myself, my progress on some curriculum, ’cause I’m going through some process of awakening or realization. And I still didn’t know what it was at that time. And I just had been reassured, I’m making good choices and that she was proud of me. It’s bringing me to tears now.

And then the rest is the experience itself. We move into education from there. That’s the personal lead in. This is who I am. I love you. I’m proud of you. And then we get into it. I’ll pause there.

Joshua: Wow. I wanna meet my mom. I wanna meet my soul mom.

I don’t know, you come in in the same batch. I don’t know. We’ll get into that. Joshua: Where did this being come from?
